  16|                                                              Vocational Projects in the District Communities
  17|                        In addition to applying their academic skills, students at Monty Tech are asked to put their vocational-
  18|                        technical skills into practice on a daily basis. Students across the twenty different vocational-technical
  19|                        education programs are building homes, reconstructing damaged properties, repairing service vehicles,
  20|                        making walkways more accessible, and performing countless community services.
  21|                        The 2016-2017 school year was a busy one for our vocational educators, as each trade aimed to provide
  22|                         practical, hands-on learning experiences for our students, while helping to improve each of the eighteen
  23|                        member communities.
  24|                         Auto Body Collision Repair Technology: The Monty Tech Auto Body shop benefitted from new
  25|                         instructional equipment this year, including a new Frame Machine. In addition, 2016-2017 marked the first
  26|                         year for graduating Seniors to receive the I-Car On-Line Pro Level One certification in refinishing and
  27|                         non- structural damage. This certification not only makes our program graduates more employable, it
  28|                         enables students to maintain the I-CAR gold class certification. Seniors also received an EPA 6H
  29|                         Certificate, while the Freshmen completed their Career Safe Online OSHA 10-hour General Industry
  30|                         Training. Throughout the year, students detailed, repaired, and repainted vehicles, fulfilling more than
  31|                         seventy requests that included: a trailer for the Fitchburg Police Department, and a van for the Worcester
  32|                         Fire Brigade. A Senior student won a gold Medal at the SkillsUSA state competition in the collision repair
  33|                         category, and qualified for the National event in June 2017. A total of eight students (four Seniors and four
  34|                         Juniors) participated in the school's popular Co-op Program, working in area auto body shops and
  35|                         businesses. (Total enrollment: 65; 40 males, 25 females)
  36|                         Automotive Technology: As in past years, the Monty Tech Automotive Technology program continues to
  37|                         service a variety of vehicles for faculty, staff, municipalities, and district residents. 2016-2017 proved to be
  38|                         a very busy year for the program, as more than four hundred vehicles were serviced by students and
  39|                                                                                              126

===PAGE 127=== (ocr, 48 lines)
   1|                           instructors. In addition to maintaining the school's fleet of vehicles throughout the year, students were asked
   2|                           to repair a Ford Excursion for the Bulldog Pride giveaway. Mr. Hobbs worked with a group of students to
   3|                           refurbish a 1966 Pontiac 2t2 so that so it could be auctioned off. Students performed very well in the
   4|                            SkillsUSA district competitions, placing first, second, and third, with hopes to improve in the coming year. A
   5|                           total of eight Senior and four Junior students participated in the Co-op Program, applying their technical skills
   6|                           in a real work setting. Finally, instructors are proud to announce that three talented program graduates are
   7|                            working in automotive repair shops in the area, demonstrating the skills they acquired at Monty Tech. (Total
   8|                            enrollment: 64; 50 males, 14 females)
   9|                            Business Technology: Students in Monty Tech's Business Technology program continue to benefit from a
  10|                           unique partnership with Workers' Credit Union. The opportunity to participate in teller training and financial
  11|                           literacy workshops has been met with great enthusiasm from our students, and the instructors continue to be
  12|                            grateful for this developing partnership. Monty Tech Business Technology instructors, in collaboration with
  13|                           their post-secondary colleagues, continue to refine and develop new articulation agreements. These agree-
  14|                           ments, with Mount Wachusett Community College and New England Institute of Technology, will provide
  15|                           qualified students with college credits at no cost to the student. Opportunities to demonstrate customer ser-
  16|                           vice, cash handling, and accounting skills are ever-present as the Business Technology students successfully
  17|                            operate an in-house retail location and greenhouse. The Gear House School Store acquired a credit card ma-
  18|                            chine, which has made a significant impact on the students' retail and customer service experience. In addi-
  19|                            tion, the camera in the Gear House was replaced, providing the instructors with improved vision on store ac-
  20|                            tivities while students are working. Eight Seniors and three Juniors were out on Co-op placements during the
  21|                           2016-2017 school year. (Total enrollment: 70; 20 males, 50 females)
  22|                            Cabinetmaking: All Monty Tech vocational instructors strive to provide Freshman exploratory programs that
  23|                           not only represent the work and skills students can expect to encounter throughout their high school experi-
  24|                            ence, but also one that piques the interest of students new to the trade. Monty Tech Cabinetmaking instructors
  25|                            do an outstanding job with the Freshmen exploratory experience, and in 2016-2017, the program reached
  26|                            capacity with students who listed Cabinetmaking as their top choice. Throughout the year, Cabinetmaking
  27|                            students designed and manufactured approximately 400 gifts for the Superintendent's dinner, manufactured
  28|                           and assembled seventy-four cabinet assemblies for the new Veterinary Science Training Center, and complet-
  29|                            ed more than thirty-five additional projects in and around the school, to include: Student Services conference
  30|                           room, Chromebook mobile charging stations, trophy Case for the Gardner Skating Rink, new signage for the
  31|                            Barre Cemetery, new lettering for the Ashby Fire Department, surfaced planed two large tree butts for the
  32|                            Mount Wachusett Community College, and play sets for Crocker Elementary School. Finally, Cabinetmaking
  33|                           had another great year of Co-op placement and continues to receive calls from area businesses in need of
  34|                            qualified employees. A total of fifteen students (seven Seniors and eight Juniors) were placed in Co-op expe-
  35|                           riences, and an additional two students earned full-time employment over the summer. (Total enrollment: 70;
  36|                           50 males, 20 females)
  37|                            Cosmetology: With a significant increase in the number of clients served this year, the Monty Tech Cos-
  38|                            metology program saw a significant revenue increase, which will be used to fund the program's much- need-
  39|                            ed consumable products. The new Salon Iris point-of-sale system was installed, which is a more user- friendly
  40|                           and cost effective program for students to train on. In an effort to support the school's commitment to com-
  41|                           munity service, the Junior students traveled to Heywood Wakefield Assisted Living Center on several occa-
  42|                            sions, providing residents with manicures, styling hair for a Senior Citizen Prom, and offered discounted ser-
  43|                           vices on "Staff Night" to fundraise for the field trip to New York City. Cosmetology students also provided
  44|                           manicure and hair services for the school's Child Care Center, serviced parents from Athol that work with
  45|                            disadvantaged children, and assisted our Drama club with hair and makeup. The program's greatest accom-
  46|                           plishment was realized when 100% of the Senior class passed the Massachusetts Cosmetology State Boards,
  47|                           earning licenses to practice. (Total enrollment: 91, 0 males, 91 females)

===PAGE 128=== (ocr, 48 lines)
   1|                            Culinary Arts: Monty Tech Culinary Arts students and instructors are always busy with the daily
   2|                            operation of the Mountain Room Restaurant, which is open for lunch from 11:30am to 1:00pm
   3|                            Monday through Friday. In addition to operating a full-service restaurant and bakery, serving 90-
   4|                            120 patrons daily, culinary students showcase their talents throughout the year, providing outstand-
   5|                            ing service at events that include the following: two Program Advisory Committee dinner meet-
   6|                            ings, four Monty Tech Foundation breakfasts, Monty Tech's Retirees holiday luncheon, a gradua-
   7|                            tion reception, the Women in Technology event, Principal and Counselors' Day, the Monty Tech
   8|                            homecoming dance, MAVA meetings, all School Committee meetings and sub- committee meet-
   9|                            ings, Gardner AARP luncheon and MHCC volunteer luncheon. Community service opportunities
  10|                            for students are always a highlight, and this year our students prepared meals for the United Way's
  11|                            "Day of Caring" event, Our Fathers House, and NEADS events. Students also participated in the
  12|                            "Puppy Love" event in which 300 cupcakes and 350 cookies were made. The Culinary Arts pro-
  13|                            gram also donated pastry and baked goods to the House of Peace and Education in Gardner. The
  14|                            program's greatest undertaking is always the Annual Superintendent's Dinner scholarship fundrais-

  17|                                                                  Co-operative Education and Student Placement
  18|                            The Cooperative Education Program is an extension of the student's technical education that com-
  19|                            bines classroom instruction with on-the-job-training. The Co-op Program provides students the op-
  20|                            portunity to further develop academic, technical and employability skills in an industry work envi-
  21|                            ronment. All students are eligible to participate in Co-op Program, provided they satisfy state and
  22|                            school grade, attendance and performance requirements. All Co-op students have completed the-
  23|                            OSHA recognized Career Safe online health and safety course.
  24|                            The Cooperative Education Program is beneficial for both student and employer. For the student, it
  25|                            is a chance to gain on-the job training that improves both their technical and employability skills.
  26|                            Additionally, making industry connections enhances post-graduate employment opportunities. The
  27|                            Co-op Program allows students to earn while they learn.
  28|                            Employer benefits include addressing workforce needs in an efficient manner. Coop students pro-
  29|                            vide a pool of temporary and potential full-time employees who are already trained, thus reducing
  30|                            employer training costs. Co-op work hours may be tailored to suit the needs of partnering employ-
  31|                            ers. During school year 2016-2017, a record number of students participated in the cooperative ed-
  32|                            ucation program. Approximately 37% of the class of 2017 earned Co-op placements, working,
  33|                           learning and applying their technical skills related in area businesses.

  19|                                                                               Student Support Services
  20|                            During the 2016-2017 school year, Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District pro-
  21|                           vided special services to approximately 300 students- measuring progress of over 200 students on
  22|                           Individual Education Plans (IEPs) and just under 100 students adhering to individualized Section 504
  23|                           plans. While the Student Support Services (SSS) Dept. encompasses special education, the depart-
  24|                           ment provides support and is available to all Monty Tech students.
  25|                           The department includes a full-time nursing staff that administers medications, performs state-
  26|                           mandated health screening exams, and provides, when necessary, health information to the special
  27|                           education team for a student's IEP meeting. The department benefits from a full-time school social
  28|                           worker that participates in departmental meetings and assists students who have needs concerning
  29|                           finances, family issues, homelessness, maternity, health issues, and proper food and clothing. The
  30|                           school is also fortunate to have on staff a full-time psychologist, whose role it is to evaluate all stu-
  31|                           dents referred for an initial evaluation or who require a three- year re-evaluation. In addition, we
  32|                           have a full-time speech language pathologist, who is available to assist students with disabilities, as-
  33|                           sess these students and consult with teachers. Our students also have access to the services of a full-
  34|                           time adjustment counselor and part-time school psychologist. All of these individuals are available
  35|                           for scheduled counseling sessions, mental health emergency treatment, medical/mental health re-
  36|                           entry and transition support and services, as well as crisis intervention.
  37|                            The school's Director of Student Support Services oversees the District's Special Education Pro-
  38|                           gram, which is reviewed annually in May, in accordance with regulatory requirements. The compre-
  39|                           hensive review and evaluation are done in collaboration with the Parent Advisory Council, and the
  40|                           results of the evaluation are used to improve the special education procedures and programs in place
  41|                           at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School.
  42|                           Technology
  43|                           The 2016-2017 school year began with a successful roll-out of the long anticipated 1:1 Chromebook
  44|                           initiative. Chromebooks were distributed to 1,436 students. Google cloud Print was added, making
  45|                           printers available to students and teachers throughout the building. Two Chromebook charging sta-
  46|                           tions were installed in the library and cafeteria allowing students to charge their devices, and stu-
  47|                           dents who may have come to school without their devices have been allowed to borrow a
  48|                                                                                              133

===PAGE 134=== (ocr, 43 lines)
   1|                           Chromebook from the library, on a daily basis.
   2|                           To support the 1:1 Chromebook initiative and other technology needs, Monty Tech has successfully
   3|                           completed a number of necessary infrastructure upgrades. With federal e-rate dollars totaling
   4|                           $77,752, the school's external bandwidth speeds were increased to 1.5GB and internal bandwidth
   5|                           was increased by 10GB. In addition to hardware, the school added the following complementary
   6|                           software: Lightspeed web filtering and Aruba ClearPass. Lightspeed web filtering is expected to
   7|                           provide safe, fast access to the Internet and includes flexible policies, social media controls, band-
   8|                           width control, malware protection, anonymous proxy detection, mobile filtering, classroom man-
   9|                           agement tools, and comprehensive reporting. Aruba ClearPass will provide strong network access
  10|                           security for monitoring wireless devices, as well as enhanced options for guest access services.
  11|                           The school also launched a Digital Citizenship course, proving lessons and guidance relating to ap-
  12|                           propriate online behavior, including cyberbullying, potential risks related to social networking sites
  13|                           and chat rooms. Students at Monty Tech are expected to demonstrate the safe and responsible use
  14|                           of technology and an understanding of security, privacy, and ethics. The district maintains Internet
  15|                           Safety information on the district website in the Parents & Students section.
  16|                                                                                 Marine Corps JROTC
  17|                           The Monty Tech Marine Corps Junior Reserve Officer Training Corps (MCJROTC) had an extraor-
  18|                           dinary school year. All program objectives for the 2016-2017 school year were achieved. Notewor-
  19|                           thy was the performance of JROTC's Cyber Security Team, which for the seventh consecutive year
  20|                           captured national recognition by placing third overall in the Air Force Association's National Cyber
  21|                           Security Competition held in Baltimore, MD.
  22|                           The 2016-2017 Corps of Cadets completed over nine thousand hours of community service
  23|                           throughout the district. The Corps conducted a major food drive with United Way, worked side-by
  24|                           -side with the Marine Corps Reserve in a national Toys for Tots campaign, and spent six weekends
  25|                           working with the local Salvation Army helping to raise over $25,000 for needy families. The cadets
  26|                           conducted their annual 10- mile "March-A- Thon" to support NEADS (National Education of As-
  27|                           sistance Dogs Services) from Princeton, MA, raising $25,000 for their Service Dogs for Veterans
  28|                           program.
  29|                           Sixty-five Monty Tech cadets attended a leadership camp at Prince William Forest, in Quantico
  30|                           VA. The camp provided cadets with individual and team development opportunities, as well as the
  31|                           opportunity to visit our nation's capital and many other historical sites.
  32|                           During the summer of 2017, our Cadet Cyber Team was again asked to support a Cyber STEM
  33|                           camp for the National Marine Corps JROTC program. The Cyber Team coordinated the 8-day
  34|                           camp that focused on defending cyber networks from attacks. The camp also offered an introduc-
  35|                           tion to robotics programming using the VEX Robotics System and computer programming using
  36|                           the CanaKit Raspberry Pi 3's. The camp was attended by 150 students, representing eighteen
  37|                           states.

===PAGE 136=== (ocr, 45 lines)
   1|                                                                       Monty Tech School of Continuing Education
   2|                             The Monty Tech School of Continuing Education continues to update and add courses that empha-
   3|                             size a commitment to excellence by offering affordable, quality, and enjoyable educational experi-
   4|                            ences. For the Fall of 2016, Monty Tech offered 65 classes with 580 registrations, and during the
   5|                             Spring 2017 semester, there were 79 postgraduate and continuing studies courses, with 857 regis-
   6|                             trations.
   7|                             The program is on track to expand the number of workforce readiness classes that provide our stu-
   8|                             dents with industry-recognized credentials, increasing our small business partnerships, and the
   9|                             number and quality of personal enrichment classes - sewing, knitting, acrylic painting and sculpt-
  10|                            ing, to name a few. Catalog distribution has almost doubled, from 80,000 to 155,000 copies, and
  11|                             the relatively new online registration feature continues to add value to our students and instructors.
  12|                             Spring 2018 will see the program's first Cosmetology courses come to fruition. This will provide
  13|                             our community members with a high-quality, affordable entry into a viable career pathway. Upon
  14|                            completion of the 14-month program, students will have the opportunity to sit for the cosmetology
  15|                            licensing exam, and begin their work in the exciting cosmetology industry.
  16|                             The continued success and sustainability of an adult education program is based on hiring quality
  17|                            instructors and meeting the needs of the community. Our goal in the coming year includes increas-
  18|                            ing our adult education course offerings, with a concentration on career programs. Stay tuned for
  19|                            Pharmacy Technician, Welding Certification Prep, Auto Damage Appraiser and Apprenticeship
  20|                             opportunities at the Monty Tech School of Continuing Education.
  21|                                                                               Practical Nursing Program
  22|                           The Practical Nursing Program is designed to prepare graduates to practice safely, ethically and in a
  23|                             caring manner for patients who are experiencing common variations in health status in diverse
  24|                            health care settings. This mission which is consistent with the philosophy and goals of the Monta-
  25|                            chusett Regional Vocational Technical School District, accomplishes the following:
  26|                           • Identifies a strong relationship between academic and vocational preparation
  27|                           • Stresses the importance of developing critical thinking skills to function safely, effec-
  28|                                 tively, and productively in an ever-changing technical and diverse society
  29|                           • Supports the maintenance of a positive and caring learning and practice environment
  30|                           The above mission and philosophy were met by the Practical Nursing program by several different
  31|                             methods.
  32|                           On June 29, 2017, a graduating class of 38 students completed the Practical Nursing Program and
  33|                            entered the nursing profession. The class achieved an initial NCLEX pass rate of 76%, with 28 of
  34|                            the 38 graduates achieving 100% pass rate for the NCLEX-PN exam. Seven graduates are waiting
  35|                            to retake their NCLEX- PN exam, and two graduates have already taken the exam again, and
  36|                             passed.
  37|                             78% of the 2017 graduates are currently employed in the health care profession throughout Mas-
  38|                            sachusetts, and many are working within the eighteen cities and towns of the Monty Tech school
  39|                            district as LPNs in various health care settings, ranging from long term care, sub-acute care to
  40|                             mental health facilities.
  41|                             78% of the 2017 graduates are currently employed in the health care profession throughout Mas-
  42|                            sachusetts, and many are working within the eighteen cities and towns of the Monty Tech school
  43|                             district as LPNs in various health care settings, ranging from long term care, sub-acute care to
  44|                            mental health facilities.
  45|                                                                                              136

===PAGE 137=== (ocr, 45 lines)
   1|                          The Monty Tech Practical Nursing Program continues to develop the "LPN to BSN Bridge" relation-
   2|                          ship with Fitchburg State University. Several 2017 graduates are pursuing seats in the LPN to BSN
   3|                          program at FSU and will be continuing their education to the Bachelors in Nursing. Many class appli-
   4|                          cants cited their attraction to Monty Tech as the opportunity to complete a ten-month accelerated pro-
   5|                          gram and then bridge to Fitchburg State University.
   6|                          Monty Tech Practical Nursing students are now completing patient scenarios in the Sim Lab on a
   7|                          weekly basis in Terms 2 and 3. The Faculty Sim team has implemented National League of Nursing
   8|                          (NLN) patient scenarios consistent with our curriculum frameworks, and have also utilized the Sim
   9|                          lab setting to instruct students in developing nursing skills that they may not be experiencing in the
  10|                          clinical setting. Instructors have successfully developed a pediatric patient scenario that is consistent
  11|                          with present student learning outcomes and the clinical pediatric setting. Similarly, instructors have
  12|                          implemented a scenario that deals with a mental health addiction client, so that our students are better
  13|                          prepared for the clinical experience at partnering institution AdCare Hospital in Worcester, MA.
  14|                          The class of 2017 performed a community service project adopting a Monty Tech family in need re-
  15|                          ferred by Student Support Services. The nursing students purchased holiday gifts and food, consistent
  16|                          with the family's needs and requests.
  17|                          The Practical Nursing Program Faculty spent the summer of 2017 reviewing the revised 2017 NCLEX
  18|                          -PN Detailed Test Plan, and the program's student outcomes, to assure alignment and compliance
  19|                          with our curriculum frameworks and content. The annual review process is an important component
  20|                          of the program, ensuring the Monty Tech Practical Nursing Program presents students with rigorous
  21|                          content, aligned with industry standards, so that our graduates are prepared to succeed not only on the
  22|                          licensing exam, but also in their chosen nursing career pathway.
  23|                          With an eye toward the program's future, fifty-one applicants were accepted to the Class of 2018, and
  24|                          forty students enrolled and will continue in the tradition of high-quality practical nursing preparation.
  25|                                                                                      Looking Ahead
  26|                          While the Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District educational community is cer-
  27|                          tainly proud of the achievements of our talented students, faculty, and staff, we continue to have an
  28|                          eye toward the future, always committed to improving our vocational and academic programming,
  29|                          strengthening key partnerships, and maintaining facilities that contribute to student success and
  30|                          achievement. As we look ahead, there are a number of programs and initiatives that we expect will
  31|                          have a positive impact on our school and students for years to come.
  32|                          Expand science offerings to include new biomedical courses: In 2016-2017 the school received a
  33|                          $99,000 grant from the Massachusetts Life Science Center, which allowed us to purchase new instruc-
  34|                          tional materials and equipment, provide the required, high quality professional development needed to
  35|                          teach new coursework, and renovate an existing science laboratory to replicate biomedical industry
  36|                          standards. Dylan Hager, a longtime science instructor at Monty Tech traveled to University of San
  37|                          Diego July of 2017, and will return July of 2018, to complete training in two Project Lead the Way
  38|                          courses, Principles of Biomedical Science and Human Body Systems.
  39|                          These courses, which will be available to students Fall 2018, are expected to engage students in a vari-
  40|                          ety of hands-on learning opportunities in a newly renovated laboratory environment that replicates
  41|                          industry standards. Students will be trained in the safe and effective use of the same tools that profes-
  42|                          sionals in hospitals and laboratories are currently using and will work together to find solutions to real
  43|                          -world problems by analyzing medical histories, reading and interpreting autopsy reports, and under-
  44|                          standing basic biology and human physiology.
  45|                                                                                              137

===PAGE 138=== (ocr, 30 lines)
   1|                            Expand vocational-technical programs to include new, Chapter 74-approved, Veterinary Science
   2|                            program: While construction and fundraising efforts for the school's new program have been un-
   3|                            derway for almost two years, to effectively bring a new program to a Massachusetts vocational-
   4|                            technical school, a comprehensive application and supporting documents must be accepted by the
   5|                             Department of Elementary and Secondary Education. During the 2017-2018 school year, school
   6|                            officials will complete the application, finalize all requirements outlined in the application, and
   7|                            anticipate being granted approval for the new secondary-level program titled Animal Science.
   8|                             This approval is given pursuant to Massachusetts General Law Chapter 74 and the Vocational
   9|                             Technical Education Regulations 603 CMR 4.00, and will enable all interested Freshman to ex-
  10|                            plore the new program. A Freshman class (class size of twenty-two students) will be accepted into
  11|                            the program January 2018.
  12|                             Expand post-secondary and continuing education opportunities: As you know, Montachusett Re-
  13|                            gional Vocational Technical School is home to a very popular evening program that includes a
  14|                            postsecondary Practical Nursing program, Plumbing and Electrical apprenticeship programs, and
  15|                            continuing education courses that range from healthcare to advanced manufacturing to cake deco-
  16|                            rating. While we are proud of our current evening programs, our goal for the 2017-2018 school
  17|                            year is to expand programs to include an entirely electronic registration and payment process, an
  18|                            updated web presence, increased enrollment, and a minimum of three new workforce develop-
  19|                            ment programs that result in an industry recognized credential or certification. Catalogs highlight-
  20|                            ing each new course will be mailed to more than 180,000 households bi- annually, in August and
  21|                             Redesign the school's website and expand marketing efforts: A Monty Tech high school experi-
  22|                            ence is an educational pathway students must choose, and to ensure that every interested student
  23|                            in our sending district has access to information needed to make sound decisions regarding his/her
  24|                            high school experience, it is imperative that the school's marketing efforts are expanded to in-
  25|                            clude a newly designed website, with descriptions of each vocational shop, academic programs,
  26|                            and admissions criteria. In addition, expanding our marketing efforts to include a social media
  27|                            presence that is current and engaging is a top priority. During the 2017-2018 school year, school
  28|                            officials anticipate launching a new school website, adding updated content to our Facebook page
  29|                            on a regular basis, and launching a school/district Twitter account.

===PAGE 146=== (ocr, 24 lines)
   1|                                                              DETERMINATION OF ASSESSMENT RATIOS
   2|                           Each Community's assessment is made up of four parts:
   3|                          ~ Required Minimum Contribution (set by the State)
   4|                          ~ Transportation & Other Operating Expenses above Minimum Net School Spending
   5|                          ~ Capital Outlay
   6|                          ~ Bonds (assessed based upon the Capital apportionment)
   7|                          IRANSPORTATION & OTHER OPERATING BUDGET - (determined by each Communities enrollment, October 1, 2017)
   8|                           Formula: The number of students from each member community enrolled at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School
   9|                                     divided by total Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School Day school enrollment of member communities
  10|                                     on October 1, 2017 equals the operating ratio.
  11|                          Example: Fitchburg students enrolled at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School on October 1, 2017 = 398.
  12|                                     Total Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School enrollment of 18 member communities on October 1, 2014 = 1,496.
  13|                                     Therefore, ratio is 398 divided by 1,496.
  14|                                                           398
  15|                                                          1496           =      0.2660                           26.60%
  16|                          CAPITAL BUDGET - (determined by each Communities school attending children, grades 1 - 12, October 1, 2017)
  17|                           Formula: The number of students from each member community enrolled in Grades 1 through 12 divided by the total number of
  18|                                     students enrolled in Grades 1 through 12 of 18 member communities equals the capital assessment ratio.
  19|                          Example: Holden students enrolled grades 1 through 12 on October 1, 2017 = 2,672.
  20|                                     Total enrollment of 18 member communities in Grades 1 through 12 on October 1, 2014 = 24,147.
  21|                                     Therefore, ratio is 2,672 divided by 24,147.
  22|                                                          3059
  23|                                                         24711                   0.1238                  =        12.38%
  24|                                                                                              146

   1|                                                                       TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS
   2|                                                                ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - MAY 6, 2017
   3|                         The Annual Town Meeting was called to order at 9:04 A.M. in the Lunenburg Middle/High School
   4|                         Auditorium. A quorum was declared present and the warrant noted as being properly served. We
   5|                         pledged allegiance; the Moderator thanked those residents serving on town boards and noted that
   6|                         more volunteers were needed to fill vacancies. Mr. Toale thanked Mr. Alonzo for his years of
   7|                         service. The Moderator then went over the grounds rules, etc.
   8|                         ARTICLE 1. To see if the Town will vote to hear and/or accept the regular written reports of the Town
   9|                         Officers and Committees; or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by Board of Selectmen)
  10|                         Board of Selectmen recommends approval. Finance Committee reports no direct financial impact.
  11|                         VOTED UNANIMOUSLY
  12|                         ARTICLE 2. To see if the Town will vote to authorize revolving funds for the Fire Department,
  13|                         Conservation Commission, the School Committee, the Library, and the Council on Aging pursuant to
  14|                         Massachusetts General Law Chapter 44, Section 53E ½ for the fiscal year beginning July 1, 2017; or take
  15|                         any other action relative thereto. Board of Selectmen and Finance Committee recommends approval.
  16|                         VOTED UNANIMOUSLY

  13|                        ARTICLE 33. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Zoning Bylaw, to add Section 4.18 Temporary
  14|                        Moratorium on the Sale and Distribution of Recreational Marijuana and further to amend the Table of
  15|                        Contents to insert Section 4.18 Temporary Moratorium; or take any action relative thereto. Board of
  16|                        Selectmen recommends approval. Finance Committee reports no direct financial impact. VOTED
  17|                        UNANIMOUSLY to insert a new Section 4.18, Temporary Moratorium on the Sale and
  18|                        Distribution of Recreational Marijuana and to amend the Table of Contents by adding the words
  19|                       "Temporary Moratorium on the Sale and Distribution of Recreational Marijuana" as follows:
  20|                        4.18 Temporary Moratorium on the Sale and Distribution of Recreational Marijuana.
  21|                        4.18.1 Purpose:
  22|                                   By vote at the State election on November 8, 2016, the voters of the Commonwealth approved a law
  23|                                  regulating the cultivation, distribution, possession and use of marijuana for recreational purposes.
  24|                                   The law provides that it is effective on December 15, 2016 and the Cannabis Control Commission
  25|                                  is currently required to issue regulations regarding implementation by March 15, 2018 (see Section
  26|                                   11 of Chapter 351 of the Acts of 2016).
  27|                                   Currently under the Zoning Bylaw, Recreational Marijuana Establishments and Marijuana
  28|                                   Retailers are not a permitted use in the Town and any regulations promulgated by the State
  29|                                   Cannabis Control Commission are expected to provide guidance to the Town in regulating
  30|                                   Recreational Marijuana Establishments and Marijuana Retailers.
  31|                                   The regulation of Recreational Marijuana Establishments and Marijuana Retailers raise novel and
  32|                                   complex legal, planning, and public safety issues and the Town needs time to study and consider
  33|                                  the regulation of Recreational Marijuana Establishments and Marijuana Retailers and address such
  34|                                   novel and complex issues, as well as to address the potential impact of the State regulations on
  35|                                   local zoning and to undertake a planning process to consider amending the Zoning Bylaw
  36|                                  regarding regulation of Recreational Marijuana Establishments and Marijuana Retailers and other
  37|                                   uses related to the regulation of recreational marijuana. The Town intends to adopt a temporary
  38|                                   moratorium on the use of land and structures in the Town for Recreational Marijuana
  39|                                   Establishments and Marijuana Retailers so as to allow the Town sufficient time to engage in a
  40|                                   planning process to address effects of such structures and uses in the Town and to adopt
  41|                                                                                              164

===PAGE 165=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                                       provisions of the Zoning Bylaw in a manner consistent with sound land use planning goals and
   2|                                       objectives.
   3|                            4.18.2 Definitions.
   4|                                       "Manufacture", to compound, blend, extract, infuse or otherwise make or prepare a marijuana
   5|                                       product.
   6|                                       "Marijuana accessories", equipment, products, devices or materials of any kind that are intended or
   7|                                       designed for use in planting, propagating, cultivating, growing, harvesting, manufacturing,
   8|                                       compounding, converting, producing, processing, preparing, testing, analyzing, packaging,
   9|                                       repackaging, storing, containing, ingesting, inhaling or otherwise introducing marijuana into the
  10|                                       human body.
  11|                                       "Marijuana cultivator", an entity licensed to cultivate, process and package marijuana, to
  12|                                       deliver marijuana to marijuana establishments and to transfer marijuana to other marijuana
  13|                                       establishments, but not to consumers.
  14|                                       "Marijuana establishment", a marijuana cultivator, marijuana testing facility, marijuana product
  15|                                       manufacturer, marijuana retailer or any other type of licensed marijuana-related business.
  16|                                       "Marijuana product manufacturer", an entity licensed to obtain, manufacture, process and package
  17|                                       marijuana and marijuana products, to deliver marijuana and marijuana products to marijuana
  18|                                       establishments and to transfer marijuana and marijuana products to other marijuana establishments,
  19|                                       but not to consumers.
  20|                                       "Marijuana products", products that have been manufactured and contain marijuana or an extract
  21|                                        rom marijuana, including concentrated forms of marijuana and products composed of marijuan
  22|                                        und other ingredients that are intended for use or consumption, including edible products, beverages
  23|                                       topical products, ointments, oils and tinctures.
  24|                                       "Marijuana testing facility", an entity licensed to test marijuana and marijuana products,
  25|                                       including certification for potency and the presence of contaminants.
  26|                                       "Marijuana retailer", an entity licensed to purchase and deliver marijuana and marijuana
  27|                                       products from marijuana establishments and to deliver, sell or otherwise transfer marijuana and
  28|                                       marijuana products to marijuana establishments and to consumers.
  29|                            4.18.3 Temporary Moratorium.
  30|                                       For the reasons set forth above and notwithstanding any other provision of the Zoning Bylaw to
  31|                                       the contrary, the Town hereby adopts a temporary moratorium on the use of land or structures for
  32|                                       Recreational Marijuana Establishments and Marijuana Retailers. The moratorium shall be in
  33|                                       effect through November 30, 2018. During the moratorium period, the Town shall undertake a
  34|                                       planning process to address the potential impacts of recreational marijuana in the Town,
  35|                                       consider the Cannabis Control Commission regulations regarding Recreational Marijuana
  36|                                       Establishments and Marijuana Retailers and related uses, , determine whether the town will
  37|                                       prohibit on-site consumption at Recreational Marijuana Establishments and Marijuana Retailers
  38|                                       and shall consider adopting new provisions of the Zoning Bylaw to address the impact and
  39|                                       operation of Recreational Marijuana Establishments and Marijuana Retailers and related uses.
  40|                                                                                              165

===PAGE 166=== (ocr, 48 lines)
   1|                               4.18.4 Severability.
   2|                                          The provisions of this Bylaw are severable. If any provision, paragraph, sentence, or clause of this
   3|                                          Bylaw or the application thereof to any person, establishment, or circumstances shall be held
   4|                                          invalid
