===PAGE 173=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|                       ARTICLE 20. To see if the Town will vote to amend the General Bylaws, by adding the following text as Chapter 204
   2|                        Sections 1 and 2; or take any action relative thereto. (Submitted by the Planning Board) Board of Selectmen recommends
   3|                       approval. Finance Committee no direct impact.
   4|                       204-1. STORMWATER MANAGEMENT - NPDES PHASE II PERMITS
   5|                        A. Purpose and intent.
   6|                       (1) Regulation of discharges to the municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) is necessary for the protection of the
   7|                              Town of Lunenburg's water bodies and groundwater, and to safeguard the public health, safety, welfare and the
   8|                              environment. Increased and contaminated stormwater runoff associated with developed land uses and the
   9|                              accompanying increase in impervious surface are major causes of impairment of water quality and flow in lakes,
  10|                              ponds, streams, rivers, wetlands, and groundwater which result in the contamination of drinking water supplies;
  11|                              erosion of stream channels; alteration or destruction of aquatic and wildlife habitat; and flooding. This bylaw
  12|                              establishes minimum stormwater management standards for the final conditions that result from development and
  13|                              redevelopment projects to minimize adverse impacts off-site and downstream which would be borne by abutters,
  14|                              townspeople and the general public.
  15|                       (2) This regulation requires local review and approval of a stormwater management plan for all development and
  16|                              redevelopment projects that disturb one acre or more. In addition to these regulations, the owner and/or developer is
  17|                              also obligated to meet the requirements of the Federal Environmental Protection Agency's (EPA) regulations for
  18|                              stormwater management.
  19|                        B. The objectives of this bylaw are:
  20|                       (1) To require practices to control the flow of stormwater from new and redeveloped sites to the Town's storm drainage
  21|                              system in order to prevent flooding and erosion;
  22|                       (2) To protect groundwater and surface water from degradation;
  23|                       (3) To promote groundwater recharge;
  24|                       (4) To prevent pollutants from entering the Town's municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) and to minimize
  25|                              discharge of pollutants from the MS4;
  26|                       (5) To ensure adequate long-term operation and maintenance of structural stormwater best management practices so that
  27|                              they work as designed;
  28|                       (6) To comply with state and federal statutes and regulations relating to stormwater discharges; and
  29|                       (7) To establish Lunenburg's legal authority to ensure compliance with the provisions of this bylaw through inspection,
  30|                              monitoring, and enforcement.
  31|                       C. Applicability. No person may undertake a construction activity following the effective date of this Bylaw, including
  32|                              clearing, grading and excavation that results in a land disturbance that will disturb equal to or greater than one acre
  33|                              of land or will disturb less than one acre of land but part of a larger common plan of development or sale that will
  34|                              ultimately disturb equal to or greater than one acre of land draining to the Town of Lunenburg's municipal separate
  35|                              storm sewer system, without a permit from the special permit granting authority. Construction activity does not
  36|                              include routine maintenance that is performed to maintain the original line and grade, hydraulic capacity or the
  37|                              original purpose of the site. Construction activities that are exempt are:
  38|                       (1) Normal maintenance and improvement of land in agricultural use as defined by the Wetlands Protection Act
  39|                              regulation, 310 CMR 10.04;
  40|                       (2) Maintenance of existing landscaping, gardens or lawn areas associated with a single-family dwelling;
  41|                                                                                              173

===PAGE 174=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                       (3) The construction of fencing that will not substantially alter existing terrain or drainage patterns;
   2|                       (4) Construction of utilities other than drainage (gas, water, electric, telephone, etc.) which will not alter terrain or
   3|                              drainage patterns;
   4|                       (5) Work activities of municipal, state or federal agencies or their agents outside of the Phase II boundaries as shown on
   5|                              the latest U.S. Environmental Protection Agency's National Pollutant Discharge Elimination System (NPDES) Phase
   6|                              Il maps.
   7|                       (6) As authorized in the Phase II Small MS4 General Permit for Massachusetts, stormwater discharges resulting from
   8|                              the activities identified in Subsection C(1) that are wholly subject to jurisdiction under the Wetlands Protection Act
   9|                              and demonstrate compliance with the Massachusetts Stormwater Management Policy as reflected in an order of
  10|                              conditions issued by the Conservation Commission are exempt from compliance with this bylaw.
  11|                        D. Permits and procedures.
  12|                       (1) The special permit granting authority (SPGA) under this bylaw shall be the Lunenburg Planning Board. Such special
  13|                             Pernic shalks Board of tea sand Betrie, in conjunction with he ostration ani as speciation.,
  14|                              are met. The SPGA shall not grant a special permit under this section unless the petitioner's application materials
  15|                              include, in the SPGA's opinion, sufficiently detailed, definite, and credible information to support positive findings
  16|                              in relation to the standards given in this section. The SPGA shall document the basis for any departures from the
  17|                       (2) The site owner or his agent shall file with the SPGA 10 copies of a completed application package for a stormwater
  18|                              management permit (SMP). Permit issuance is required prior to any site-altering activity. While the applicant can be
  19|                              a representative, the permittee must be the owner of the site. The SMP application package shall include:
  20|                       (a) A completed application form with original signatures of all owners;
  21|                       (b) Ten copies of the stormwater management plan and project description as specified in Subsection E(1);
  22|                       (c) Ten copies of the operation and maintenance plan as required by Subsection F of this bylaw;
  23|                       (d) Payment of the application and review fees.
  24|                       (3) Entry. Filing and application for a permit grants the SPGA, or its agent, permission to enter the site to verify the
  25|                              information in the application and to inspect for compliance with the resulting permit.
  26|                       (4) Other boards. The SPGA shall give one copy of the application package to the designated technical reviewers, which
  27|                             include the Conservation Commission, Department of Public Works, and Building Commissioner, for the purpose of
  28|                              reviewing the permit application. The SPA may also engage the services of a peer review engineer at its discretion.
  29|                       (5) Fee structure. The SPGA shall obtain with each submission an application fee established by the SPGA. The SPGA
  30|                              is authorized to retain a registered professional engineer or other professional consultant to advise the SPGA on any
  31|                              or all aspects of these plans. Applicants must pay review fees before the review process may begin.
  32|                       (6) Actions. The SPGA's action, rendered in writing, shall consist of either:
  33|                       (a) Approval of the stormwater management permit application based upon determination that the proposed plan meets
  34|                              the standards in Subsection E(2) and will adequately protect the water resources of the community and is in
  35|                              compliance with the requirements set forth in this bylaw;
  36|                       (b) Approval of the stormwater management permit application subject to any conditions, modifications or restrictions
  37|                              required by the SPGA which will ensure that the project meets the standards in Subsection E(2) and adequately
  38|                              protects water resources, as set forth in this bylaw;
  39|                                                                                              174

===PAGE 175=== (ocr, 35 lines)
   1|                       (c) Disapproval of the stormwater management permit application based upon a determination that the proposed plan, as
   2|                              submitted, does not meet the standards in Subsection E(2) or adequately protect water resources, as set forth in this
   3|                              bylaw.
   4|                       (7) Project completion. At completion of the project, the permittee shall submit as-built record drawings of all structural
   5|                              stormwater controls and treatment best management practices required for the site. The as-built drawing shall show
   6|                              deviations from the approved plans, if any, and be certified by a registered professional engineer.
   7|                        E. Contents of stormwater management plan.
   8|                       (1) Application. The stormwater management plan shall contain sufficient information for the SPGA to evaluate the
   9|                              environmental impact, effectiveness, and acceptability of the measures proposed by the applicant for reducing
  10|                              adverse impacts from stormwater. The plan shall be designed to meet the Massachusetts stormwater management
  11|                              standards as set forth in Subsection E(2) below and the current edition of the Department of Environmental
  12|                              Protection guidelines and policies. The plan shall be designed to also meet the policy standards of the SPGA. The
  13|                              stormwater management plan shall fully describe the project in drawings, and narrative. It shall include:
  14|                       (a) Locus map;
  15|                       (b) The existing zoning, and land use at the site;
  16|                       (c) The proposed land use;
  17|                       (d) The location(s) of existing and proposed easements;
  18|                       (e) The location of existing and proposed utilities;
  19|                       (t) The site's existing and proposed topography, with contours at two-foot intervals;
  20|                       (g) The existing site hydrology;
  21|                       (h) A description and delineation of existing stormwater conveyances, impoundments, and wetlands on or adjacent to
  22|                              the site or into which stormwater flows;
  23|                       (i) A delineation of one-hundred-year floodplains, if applicable;
  24|                       (i) Estimated high groundwater elevation in areas to be used for stormwater retention, detention, or infiltration;
  25|                       (k) The existing and proposed vegetation and ground surfaces, with runoff coefficient for each;
  26|                       (1) A drainage area map showing pre- and post-construction watershed boundaries, drainage area and stormwater flow
  27|                              paths;
  28|                       (m) A description and drawings of all components of the proposed drainage system, including:
  29|                       [1] Locations, cross sections, and profiles of all brooks, streams, drainage swales and their method of stabilization,;
  30|                       [2] All measures for the detention, retention, or infiltration of water;
  31|                       [3] All measures for the protection of water quality;
  32|                       [4] The structural details for all components of the proposed drainage systems and stormwater management facilities;
  33|                       [5] Notes on drawings specifying materials to be used, construction specifications, and typicals; and
  34|                       [6] Expected hydrology, with supporting calculations;
  35|                                                                                              175

===PAGE 176=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                       (n) Proposed improvements, including location of buildings or other structures, impervious surfaces, and drainage
   2|                              facilities, if applicable;
   3|                       (0) Timing, schedules, and sequence of development, including clearing, stripping, rough grading, construction, final
   4|                              grading, and vegetative stabilization;
   5|                       (p) A maintenance schedule for the period of construction; and
   6|                       (q) Any other information requested by the SPGA.
   7|                       (2) Standards. Projects shall meet the standards of the Massachusetts Stormwater Management Policy, which are as
   8|                              follows:
   9|                       (a) No new stormwater conveyances (e.g., outfalls) may discharge untreated stormwater directly to or cause erosion in
  10|                              wetlands or water of the commonwealth.
  11|                           Stormwater management systems must be designed so that post-development peak discharges rates do not exceed
  12|                              pre-development peak discharge rates.
  13|                       (c) Loss of annual recharge to groundwater should be minimized through the use of infiltration measures to the
  14|                              maximum extent practicable. The annual recharge from the post-development site should approximate the annual
  15|                              recharge rate from the pre-development or existing site conditions, based on soil types.
  16|                       (d) For new development, stormwater management systems must be designed to remove 80% of the average annual load
  17|                             (post-development conditions) of total suspended solids (TSS). It is presumed that this standard is met when:
  18|                       [1] Suitable nonstructural practices for source control and pollution prevention are implemented;
  19|                       [2] Stormwater management best management practices (BMPs) are sized to capture the prescribed runoff volume; and
  20|                       13]  Stormwater management BMPs are maintained as designed.
  21|                       (e) Stormwater discharges from areas with higher potential pollutant loads require the use of specific stormwater
  22|                              management BMPs. The use of infiltration practices without pretreatment is prohibited.
  23|                       (f) Stormwater discharges to critical areas must utilize certain stormwater management BMPs approved for critical
  24|                              areas (see MA DEP's Stormwater Management Volume I: Stormwater Policy Handbook). Critical areas are
  25|                              outstanding resource waters (ORWs), cold-water fisheries, and recharge areas for public water supplies.
  26|                       (g) Redevelopment of previously developed sites must meet the stormwater management standards to the maximum
  27|                              extent practicable. However, if it is not practicable to meet all the standards, new (retrofitted or expanded)
  28|                              stormwater management systems must be designed to improve existing conditions.
  29|                       (h)    Erosion and sediment controls must be implemented to prevent impacts during disturbance and construction
  30|                              activities.
  31|                       (i) All stormwater management systems must have an operation and maintenance plan to ensure that systems function
  32|                              as designed.
  33|                       (i) When one or more of the standards cannot be met, an applicant may demonstrate that an equivalent level of
  34|                              environmental protection will be provided.
  35|                       (3)      Project changes. The permittee, or its agent, shall notify the SPGA in writing of any change or alteration of a
  36|                       land-disturbing activity authorized in a stormwater management permit before any change or alteration occurs. If the
  37|                       SPGA determines that the change or alteration is significant, based on the design standards listed in Subsection E(2) and
  38|                       accepted construction practices, the SPA may require that an amended stormwater management permit
  39|                                                                                              176

===PAGE 177=== (ocr, 37 lines)
   1|                        application be filed and a public hearing held. If any change or deviation from the stormwater management permit occurs
   2|                              during a project, the SPGA may require the installation of interim measures before approving the change.
   3|                        F. Operation and maintenance plans. An operation and maintenance plan (O&M plan) is required at the time of
   4|                              application for all projects. The maintenance plan shall be designed to ensure compliance with the permit, this bylaw
   5|                              and that the Massachusetts Surface Water Quality Standards, 314 CMR 4.00 are met in all seasons and throughout
   6|                              the life of the system. The operation and maintenance plan shall remain on file with the SPGA and shall be an
   7|                               ongoing requirement. The O&M plan shall include:
   8|                        (1) The name(s) of the owners) for all components of the system.
   9|                        (2) Maintenance agreements that specify:
  10|                        (a) The names and addresses of the person(s) responsible for operation and maintenance.
  11|                        (b) The person(s) responsible for financing maintenance and emergency repairs.
  12|                       (3) Maintenance schedule for all drainage structures, including swales and ponds.
  13|                       (4) List of easements, with the purpose and location of each.
  14|                        (5) The signature(s) of the owners).
  15|                        (6) Stormwater management easements).
  16|                       (a)  Stormwater management easements shall be provided by the property owners) as necessary for:
  17|                        [1] Access for facility inspections and maintenance.
  18|                       12] Preservation of stormwater runoff conveyance, infiltration, and detention areas and facilities, including flood routes
  19|                              for the one-hundred-year storm event.
  20|                       [3] Direct maintenance access by heavy equipment to structures requiring regular cleanout.
  21|                       (b)  The purpose of each easement shall be specified in the maintenance agreement signed by the property owner.
  22|                        (c) Stormwater management easements are required for all areas used for off-site stormwater control, unless a waiver is
  23|                              granted by the SPGA.
  24|                       (d)    Easements shall be recorded with the Worcester County Registry of Deeds prior to issuance of a certificate of
  25|                              completion by the SPGA.
  26|                        (7) Changes to operation and maintenance plans.
  27|                        (a) The owners) of the stormwater management system must notify the SPGA of changes in ownership or assignment
  28|                               of financial responsibility.
  29|                       (b) The maintenance schedule in the maintenance agreement may be amended to achieve the purposes of this bylaw by
  30|                              mutual agreement of the SPGA and the responsible parties. Amendments must be in writing and signed by all
  31|                              responsible parties. Responsible parties shall include the owners), persons with financial responsibility, and persons
  32|                               with operational responsibility.
  33|                        G.      Surety. The SPA may require the permittee to post, before the start of land disturbance or construction activity,
  34|                        a surety bond, cash, or other acceptable security. The form of the bond shall be approved by Town Counsel, and be in an
  35|                        amount deemed sufficient by the SPGA to ensure that the work will be completed in accordance with the permit. If the
  36|                        project is phased, the SPA may release part of the bond as each phase is completed in compliance with the
  37|                                                                                              177

===PAGE 178=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                       permit, but the bond may not be fully released until the SPGA has received the final inspection report as required by
   2|                              Subsection H and issued a certificate of completion.
   3|                       H. Inspections.
   4|                       (1) At the discretion of the SPGA, periodic inspections of the stormwater management system construction may be
   5|                              conducted by the Town via the Planning Board, Conservation Commission, DPW or a professional engineer
   6|                              approved by the SPGA. Written reports shall include:
   7|                       (a) Inspection date and location.
   8|                       (b) Evaluation of compliance with the stormwater permit.
   9|                       (c) Any variations from approved specifications or any violations of the stormwater management plan.
  10|                       (2) At a minimum, the SPGA or its designee may inspect the project site at the following stages:
  11|                       (a) Initial site inspection: prior to approval of any plan.
  12|                       (b) Erosion control inspection: to ensure erosion control practices are in accord with the filed plan.
  13|                       (c) Bury inspection: prior to backfilling of any underground drainage stormwater conveyance structures.
  14|                             Final inspection. After the stormwater management system has been constructed and before the surety has been
  15|                              released, the applicant must submit a record plan detailing the actual stormwater management system as installed.
  16|                              The SPGA or its agent may inspect the system to confirm its "as-built" features. This inspector may also evaluate the
  17|                              effectiveness of the system in an actual storm. If the inspector finds the system to be adequate he shall so report to
  18|                              the SPGA, which will issue a certificate of completion. As-built plans shall be full-size plans that include all final
  19|                              grades, prepared by a professional engineer. All changes to project design should be clearly depicted on the as-built
  20|                              plans.
  21|                       (e)   If the system is found to be inadequate by virtue of physical evidence of operational failure, even though it was built
  22|                             as called for in the stormwater management plan, it shall be corrected by the permittee before the performance
  23|                              guarantee is released. If the permittee fails to act, the Town of Lunenburg may use the surety bond to complete the
  24|                              work. If the system does not comply with the plan, the permittee shall be notified in writing of the violation and the
  25|                              required corrective actions. A stop-work order shall be issued until any violations are corrected and all work
  26|                              previously completed has received approval by the SPGA.
  27|                       I.     Waivers.
  28|                       (1) The SPGA may waive strict compliance with any requirement of this bylaw or the rules and regulations promulgated
  29|                              hereunder, where such action:
  30|                       (a) Is allowed by federal, state and local statutes and/or regulations;
  31|                       (b) Is in the public interest; and
  32|                       (c) Is not inconsistent with the purpose and intent of this bylaw.
  33|                       (2) Any applicant may submit a written request to be granted such a waiver. Such a request shall be accompanied by an
  34|                              explanation or documentation supporting the waiver request and demonstrating that strict application of the bylaws
  35|                              does not further the purposes or objectives of this bylaw.
  36|                       (3) All waiver requests shall be discussed and voted on at the public hearing for the project.
  37|                       (4) If, in the SPGA's opinion, additional time or information is required for review of a waiver request, the SPGA
  38|                       may
  39|                                                                                              178

===PAGE 179=== (ocr, 42 lines)
   1|                       continue the hearing to a date certain announced at the meeting. In the event the applicant objects to a continuance, or fails
   2|                              to provide requested information, the waiver request shall be denied.
   3|                       J. Certificate of completion. The SPGA will issue a letter certifying completion upon receipt and approval of the final
   4|                              inspection reports and/or upon otherwise determining that all work of the permit has been satisfactorily completed in
   5|                              conformance with this bylaw.
   6|                       K. Enforcement.
   7|                       (1) Enforcement agents. The Building Commissioner, in conjunction with the Planning Board, Conservation
   8|                              Commission and DPW Director, shall enforce this bylaw, regulations, orders, violation notices, and enforcement
   9|                              orders, and may pursue all civil and criminal remedies for such violations.
  10|                       L. Civil relief. If a person violates the provisions of this bylaw, regulations, a permit, notice, or order issued thereunder,
  11|                              the Planning Board, through the Building Commissioner, may seek injunctive relief in a court of competent
  12|                             jurisdiction restraining the person from activities which would create further violations or compelling the person to
  13|                              perform abatement or remediation of the violation.
  14|                       M. Orders.
  15|                       (1) The Planning Board, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission and DPW Director, may issue a written
  16|                              order to the Building Commissioner to enforce the provisions of this bylaw or the regulations thereunder, which may
  17|                       (a)  Elimination of illicit connections or discharges to the MS4;
  18|                       (b) Performance of monitoring, analyses, and reporting;
  19|                       (c) That unlawful discharges, practices, or operations shall cease and desist;
  20|                       (d) Remediation of contamination in connection therewith.
  21|                       (2) If the Building Commissioner, in conjunction with the Planning Board, Conservation Commission and DPW
  22|                              Director, determines that abatement or remediation of contamination is required, the order shall set forth a deadline
  23|                              by which such abatement or remediation must be completed. Said order shall further advise that, should the violator
  24|                              or property owner fail to abate or perform remediation within the specified deadline, the Town may, at its option,
  25|                              undertake such work, and expenses thereof shall be charged to the violator.
  26|                       (3) Within 30 days after completing all measures necessary to abate the violation or to perform remediation, the violator
  27|                              and the property owner will be notified of the cost incurred by the Town, including administrative costs. The violator
  28|                              or property owner may file a written protest objecting to the amount or basis of costs with the Planning Board within
  29|                              30 days of receipt of the notification of the cost incurred. If the amount due is not received by the expiration of the
  30|                              time in which to file a protest or within 30 days following a decision of the Planning Board affirming or reducing the
  31|                              costs, or from a final decision of a court of competent jurisdiction, the costs shall become a special assessment
  32|                              against the property owner and shall constitute a lien on the owner's property for the amount of said costs. Interest
  33|                              shall begin to accrue on any unpaid costs at the statutory rate provided in MGL c. 59, § 57 after the 31st day at
  34|                              which the costs first become due.
  35|                       N. Noncriminal disposition; right of entry.
  36|                       (1) As an alternative to criminal prosecution or civil action, the Planning Board may elect, through the Building
  37|                       Commissioner, to utilize the noncriminal disposition procedure set forth in MGL c. 40, § 21D, and Chapter 1, Article I, of
  38|                       the General Bylaws of the Town, in which case the Planning Board, its authorized agents and the Building Commissioner
  39|                       of the Town shall be the enforcing person. The penalty for the first violation shall be $25. The penalty for the second
  40|                       violation shall be $50. The penalty for the third violation shall be $100 and $200 for the fourth and each subsequent
  41|                       violation. Each day or part thereof that such violation occurs or continues shall constitute a separate
  42|                                                                                              179

===PAGE 180=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                       offense.
   2|                       (2) Entry to perform duties under this bylaw. To the extent permitted by state law, or if authorized by the owner or other
   3|                              party in control of the property, the Planning Board, Its agents, and employees may enter upon privately owned
   4|                             property for the purpose of performing their duties under this bylaw and regulations and may make or cause to be
   5|                              made such examination surveys or sampling as the Planning Board deems reasonably necessary.
   6|                       O. Appeals. The decisions or orders of the Planning Board, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission and
   7|                              DPW, shall be final. Further relief shall be to a court of competent jurisdiction.
   8|                       P. Remedies not exclusive. The remedies listed in this bylaw are not exclusive of any other remedies available under
   9|                              any applicable federal, state or local law.
  10|                       Q. Severability. The invalidity of any section, provision, paragraph, sentence, or clause of these regulations shall not
  11|                              invalidate any section, provision, paragraph, sentence, or clause thereof, nor shall it invalidate any permit or
  12|                              determination that previously has been issued.
  13|                       204-2 Discharges to municipal storm sewer system and waters of commonwealth; NPDES Phase I|
  14|                       permits.
  15|                       A.    Purpose.
  16|                       (1) Increased and contaminated stormwater runoff is a major cause of impairment of water quality and flow in lakes,
  17|                              ponds, streams, rivers, wetlands and groundwater; contamination of drinking water supplies; alteration or destruction
  18|                              of aquatic and wildlife habitat; flooding.
  19|                           Regulation of illicit connections and discharges to the municipal storm sewer system is necessary for the protection
  20|                              of the Town's water bodies and groundwater, and to safeguard the public health, safety, welfare and the
  21|                              environment.
  22|                       (3) The objectives of this bylaw are:
  23|                       (a) To prevent pollutants from entering the Town's municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4);
  24|                       (b) To prohibit illicit connections and unauthorized discharges to the MS4;
  25|                       (c) To require the removal of all such illicit connections;
  26|                       (d) To comply with state and federal statutes and regulations relating to stormwater discharges; and
  27|                       (e) To establish the legal authority to ensure compliance with the provisions of this bylaw through inspection,
  28|                              monitoring, and enforcement.
  29|                       B. Definitions. For the purposes of this bylaw, the following shall mean:
  30|                       AUTHORIZED ENFORCEMENT AGENCY
  31|                              The Planning Board hereafter the "Board"), its employees or agents designated to administer/enforce this bylaw.
  32|                       BEST MANAGEMENT PRACTICE (BMP)
  33|                              An activity, procedure, restraint, or structural improvement that helps to reduce the quantity or improve the quality
  34|                              of stormwater runoff.
  35|                       CLEAN WATER ACT
  36|                              The Federal Water Pollution Control Act (33 U.S.C. § 1251 et seq.) as hereafter amended.
  37|                       DISCHARGE OF POLLUTANTS
  38|                       The addition from any source of any pollutant or combination of pollutants into the municipal storm sewer system or
  39|                                                                                              180

===PAGE 181=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|                              into the waters of the United States or commonwealth from any source.
   2|                       GROUNDWATER
   3|                              Water beneath the surface of the ground.
   4|                       ILLICIT CONNECTION
   5|                              A surface or subsurface sewer or conveyance, which allows an illicit discharge into the municipal storm sewer
   6|                              system, including without limitation sewage, process wastewater, or wash water and any connections from indoor
   7|                              drains, sinks, or toilets, regardless of whether said connection was previously allowed, permitted, or approved before
   8|                              the effective date of this bylaw.
   9|                       ILLICIT DISCHARGE
  10|                              Direct or indirect discharge to the municipal storm sewer system that is not composed entirely of stormwater, except
  11|                              as exempted in Subsection H. The term does not include a discharge in compliance with an NPDES stormwater
  12|                              discharge permit or a surface water discharge permit, or resulting from fire-fighting activities exempted pursuant to
  13|                              Subsection H of this bylaw.
  14|                       IMPERVIOUS SURFACE
  15|                              Any material or structure on or above the ground that prevents water infiltrating the underlying soil. Impervious
  16|                              surface includes without limitation roads, paved parking lots, sidewalks, and rooftops.
  17|                       MUNICIPAL SEPARATE STORM DRAIN SYSTEM (MS4) or MUNICIPAL STORM SEWER SYSTEM
  18|                               The system of conveyances designed or used for collecting or conveying stormwater, including any road with
  19|                               Irainage system, street, gutter, curb, inlet, piped storm drain, pumping facility, retention or detention basin, natura
  20|                              or man-made or altered drainage channel, reservoir, and other drainage structure that together comprise the storm
  21|                              drainage system owned or operated by the Town of Lunenburg.
  22|                       NATIONAL POLLUTANT DISCHARGE ELIMINATION SYSTEM (NDES) STORMWATER DISCHARGE
  23|                       PERMIT
  24|                              A permit issued by United States Environmental Protection Agency or jointly with the state that authorizes the
  25|                              discharge of pollutants to waters of the United States.
  26|                       NON-STORMWATER DISCHARGE
  27|                              Discharge to the municipal storm sewer system not composed entirely of stormwater.
  28|                       PERSON
  29|                              An individual, partnership, association, firm, company, trust, corporation, agency, authority, department or political
  30|                              subdivision of the commonwealth or the federal government, to the extent permitted by law, and any officer,
  31|                              employee, or agent of such person.
  32|                       POLLUTANT
  33|                              Any element or property of sewage, agricultural, industrial or commercial waste, runoff, leachate, heated effluent, or
  34|                              other matter, whether originating at a point or nonpoint source, that is or may be introduced into any sewage
  35|                              treatment works or waters of the commonwealth. Pollutants may include but are not limited to:
  36|                       (1) Paints, varnishes, and solvents;
  37|                       (2) Oil and other automotive fluids;
  38|                       (3) Nonhazardous liquid and solid wastes and yard wastes;
  39|                       (4) Refuse, rubbish, garbage, litter, or other discarded or abandoned objects, ordnances, accumulations and floatables;
  40|                       (5) Pesticides, herbicides, and fertilizers;
  41|                                                                                              181

===PAGE 182=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                       (6) Hazardous materials and wastes; sewage, fecal coliform and pathogens;
   2|                       (7) Dissolved and particulate metals;
   3|                       (8) Animal wastes;
   4|                       (9) Rock, sand, salt, soils;
   5|                       (10) Construction wastes and residues;
   6|                       (11) Noxious or offensive matter of any kind.
   7|                       PROCESS WASTEWATER
   8|                              Water which during manufacturing or processing comes into direct contact with or results from the production or use
   9|                              of any material, intermediate product, finished product, or waste product.
  10|                       RECHARGE
  11|                              The process by which groundwater is replenished by precipitation through the percolation of runoff and surface
  12|                              water through the soil.
  13|                       STORMWATER
  14|                              Stormwater runoff, snow melt runoff, and surface water runoff and drainage.
  15|                       SURFACE WATER DISCHARGE PERMIT
  16|                              A permit issued by the Department of Environmental Protection (DEP) pursuant to 314 CMR 3.00 that authorizes
  17|                              the discharge of pollutants to waters of the Commonwealth of Massachusetts.
  18|                       TOXIC OR HAZARDOUS MATERIAL OR WASTE
  19|                              Any material which, because of its quantity, concentration, chemical, corrosive, flammable, reactive, toxic,
  20|                              infectious or radioactive characteristics, either separately or in combination with any substance or substances,
  21|                              constitutes a present or potential threat to human health, safety, welfare, or to the environment. Toxic or hazardous
  22|                              materials include any synthetic organic chemical, petroleum product, heavy metal, radioactive or infectious waste,
  23|                              acid and alkali, and any substance defined as toxic or hazardous under MGL c. 21C and c. 21E, and the regulations
  24|                              at 310 CMR 30.000 and 310 CMR 40.0000.
  25|                       WASTEWATER
  26|                              Any sanitary waste, sludge, or septic tank or cesspool overflow, and water that during manufacturing, cleaning or
  27|                              processing, comes into direct contact with or results from the production or use of any raw material, intermediate
  28|                              product, finished product, byproduct or waste product.
  29|                       WATERCOURSE
  30|                             A natural or man-made channel through which water flows or a stream of water, including a river, brook or
  31|                              underground stream.
  32|                       WATERS OF THE COMMONWEALTH
  33|                              All waters within the jurisdiction of the commonwealth, including, without limitation, rivers, streams, lakes, ponds,
  34|                              springs, impoundments, estuaries, wetlands, and groundwater.
  35|                       C. Applicability. This bylaw shall apply to flows entering the municipally owned storm sewerage system and waters of
  36|                             the commonwealth.
  37|                         . Authority. This bylaw is adopted pursuant to the regulations of the Federal Clean Water Act found at 40 CFR 122.3
  38|                               nd the Phase II ruling from the Environmental Protection Agency found in the December 8, 1999 Federal Registe
  39|                                Responsibility for administration. The Planning Board, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission,
  40|                                                                                              182

===PAGE 183=== (ocr, 35 lines)
   1|                       Department of Public Works Director and Building Commissioner, shall administer, implement and enforce this bylaw.
   2|                              Any powers granted to or duties imposed upon the above may be delegated in writing by the above to employees or
   3|                              agents of the above.
   4|                       F. Regulations. The Planning Board may promulgate rules and regulations to effectuate the purposes of this bylaw.
   5|                              Failure by the Planning Board to promulgate such rules and regulations shall not have the effect of suspending or
   6|                              invalidating this bylaw.
   7|                        G. Prohibited activities.
   8|                       (1) Illicit discharges. No person shall dump, discharge, cause or allow to be discharged any pollutant or non-stormwater
   9|                              discharge into the municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4), into a watercourse, or into the waters of the
  10|                       (2) Illicit connections. No person shall construct, use, allow, maintain or continue any illicit connection to the municipal
  11|                              storm sewer system, regardless of whether the connection was permissible under applicable law, regulation or
  12|                              custom at the time of connection.
  13|                       (3) Obstruction of municipal storm sewer system. No person shall obstruct or interfere with the normal flow of
  14|                              stormwater into or out of the municipal storm sewer system without prior written approval from the Planning Board.
  15|                       H. Exemptions:
  16|                       (1) Discharge or flow resulting from fire-fighting activities.
  17|                       (2) The following non-stormwater discharges or flows are exempt from the prohibition of non-stormwaters, provided
  18|                              that the source is not a significant contributor of a pollutant to the municipal storm sewer system:
  19|                       (a) Water line flushing;
  20|                       (b) Flow from potable water sources;
  21|                       (c) Springs;
  22|                       (d) Natural flow from riparian habitats and wetlands;
  23|                       (e) Diverted stream flow;
  24|                       (f) Rising groundwater;
  25|                       (g) Uncontaminated groundwater infiltration as defined in 40 CFR 35.2005(20), or uncontaminated pumped
  26|                              groundwater;
  27|                       (h) Water from exterior foundation drains, footing drains (not including active groundwater dewatering systems), crawl
  28|                              space pumps, or air conditioning condensation;
  29|                       (i) Discharge from landscape irrigation or lawn watering;
  30|                       ) Water from individual residential car washing;
  31|                       (k) Discharge from dechlorinated swimming pool water (less than one ppm chlorine), provided the water is allowed to
  32|                              stand for one week prior to draining and the pool is drained in such a way as not to cause a nuisance;
  33|                       (1) Discharge from street sweeping;
  34|                       (m) Dye testing, provided verbal notification is given to the Planning Board prior to the time of the test;
  35|                                                                                              183

===PAGE 184=== (ocr, 44 lines)
   1|                       (n) Non-stormwater discharge permitted under an NPDES permit or a surface water discharge permit, waiver, or waste
   2|                              discharge order administered under the authority of the United States Environmental Protection Agency or the
   3|                              Department of Environmental Protection, provided that the discharge is in full compliance with the requirements of
   4|                              the permit, waiver, or order and applicable laws and regulations;
   5|                       (o) Discharge for which advanced written approval is received from the Planning Board as necessary to protect public
   6|                              health, safety, welfare or the environment.
   7|                       (3) Discharge or flow that results from exigent conditions and occurs during a state of emergency declared by any
   8|                              agency of the federal or state government, or by the Town of Lunenburg Town Manager, the Planning Board or the
   9|                              Board of Health.
  10|                       I.     Emergency suspension of storm sewerage system access. The Planning Board may suspend municipal storm sewer
  11|                              system access to any person or property without prior written notice when such suspension is necessary to stop an
  12|                             actual or threatened discharge of pollutants that presents imminent risk of harm to the public health, safety, welfare
  13|                              or the environment. In the event any person fails to comply with an emergency suspension order, the authorized
  14|                              enforcement agency may take all reasonable steps to prevent or minimize harm to the public health, safety, welfare
  15|                              or the environment.
  16|                       J.     Notification of spills. Notwithstanding other requirements of local, state or federal law, as soon as a person
  17|                              responsible for a facility or operation, or responsible for emergency response for a facility or operation has
  18|                              information of or suspects a release of materials at that facility or operation resulting in or which may result in
  19|                              discharge of pollutants to the municipal drainage system or waters of the commonwealth, the person shall take all
  20|                              necessary steps to ensure containment and cleanup of the release. In the event of a release of oil or hazardous
  21|                              materials, the person shall immediately notify the municipal fire and police departments and the Planning Board and
  22|                              the Lunenburg Board of Health. In the event of a release of nonhazardous material, the reporting person shall notify
  23|                              the Planning Board no later than the next business day. The reporting person shall provide to the Planning Board
  24|                              written confirmation of all telephone, facsimile or in-person notifications within three business days thereafter. If the
  25|                              discharge of prohibited materials is from a commercial or industrial facility, the facility owner or operator of the
  26|                              facility shall retain on-site a written record of the discharge and the actions taken to prevent its recurrence. Such
  27|                              records shall be retained for at least three years.
  28|                       K. Enforcement.
  29|                       (1) The Building Commissioner, in conjunction with the Planning Board, Conservation Commission and DPW Director,
  30|                              shall enforce this bylaw, regulations, orders, violation notices, and enforcement orders, and may pursue all civil and
  31|                              criminal remedies for such violations.
  32|                       (2) Civil relief. If a person violates the provisions of this bylaw, regulations, permit, notice, or order issued thereunder,
  33|                              the Planning Board, through the Building Commissioner, may seek injunctive relief in a court of competent
  34|                             jurisdiction restraining the person from activities which would create further violations or compelling the person to
  35|                              perform abatement or remediation of the violation.
  36|                       (3) Orders.
  37|                       (a) The Planning Board, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission and DPW Director, may issue a written
  38|                              order to the Building Commissioner to enforce the provisions of this bylaw or the regulations thereunder, which may
  39|                              include:
  40|                       [1] Elimination of illicit connections or discharges to the MS4;
  41|                       [2] Performance of monitoring, analyses, and reporting;
  42|                       [3] That unlawful discharges, practices, or operations shall cease and desist; and
  43|                       [4] Remediation of contamination in connection therewith.
  44|                                                                                              184

===PAGE 185=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                       (b) If the Building Commissioner, in conjunction with Planning Board, Conservation Commission and DPW Director,
   2|                              determines that abatement or remediation of contamination is required, the order shall set forth a deadline by which
   3|                              such abatement or remediation must be completed. Said order shall further advise that, should the violator or
   4|                              property owner fail to abate or perform remediation within the specified deadline, the Town may, at its option,
   5|                              undertake such work, and expenses thereof shall be charged to the violator.
   6|                       (4) Costs. Within 30 days after completing all measures necessary to abate the violation or to perform remediation, the
   7|                              violator and the property owner will be notified of the costs incurred by the Town, including administrative costs.
   8|                              The violator or property owner may file a written protest objecting to the amount or basis of costs with the Planning
   9|                              Board within 30 days of receipt of the notification of the costs incurred. If the amount due is not received by the
  10|                              expiration of the time in which to file a protest or within 30 days following a decision of the Planning Board
  11|                              affirming or reducing the costs, or from a final decision of a court of competent jurisdiction, the costs shall become a
  12|                              special assessment against the property owner and shall constitute a lien on the owner's property for the amount of
  13|                              said costs. Interest shall begin to accrue on any unpaid costs at the statutory rate provided in MGL c. 59, § 57 after
  14|                              the 31st day at which the costs first become due.
  15|                       (5) Noncriminal disposition. As an alternative to criminal prosecution or civil action, the Planning Board may elect,
  16|                              through the Building Commissioner, to utilize the noncriminal disposition procedure set forth in MGL c. 40, 8 21D,
  17|                              in which case the Building Commissioner of the Town shall be the enforcing person. The penalty for the first
  18|                              violation shall be $25. The penalty for the second violation shall be $50. The penalty for the third violation shall be
  19|                              $100. The penalty for the fourth violation and subsequent violations shall be $200. Each day or part thereof that such
  20|                              violation occurs or continues shall constitute a separate offense.
  21|                       (6) Entry to perform duties. Under this bylaw, to the extent permitted by state law, or if authorized by the owner or other
  22|                              party in control of the property, the Planning Board, its agents, and employees may enter upon privately owned
  23|                              property for the purpose of performing their duties under this bylaw and regulations and may make or cause to be
  24|                              made such examinations, surveys or sampling as the Planning Board deems reasonably necessary.
  25|                       (7) Appeals. The decisions or orders of the Planning Board, Conservation Commission and DPW shall be final. Further
  26|                              relief shall be to a court of competent jurisdiction.
  27|                       (8) Remedies not exclusive. The remedies listed in this bylaw are not exclusive of any other remedies available under
  28|                              any applicable federal, state or local law.
  29|                        L. Severability. The provisions of this bylaw are hereby declared to be severable. If any provision, paragraph, sentence,
  30|                              or clause of this bylaw or the application thereof to any person, establishment, or circumstances shall be held invalid,
  31|                              such invalidity shall not affect the other provisions or application of this bylaw.
  32|                       M. Transitional provisions. Residential property owners shall have 180 days from the effective date of the bylaw to
  33|                              comply with its provisions or petition the Planning Board for an extension, provided good cause is shown for the
  34|                              failure to comply with the bylaw during the specified period. All other property owners shall have 180 days from the
  35|                              effective date of the bylaw to comply with its provisions or petition the Planning Board for an extension, provided
  36|                              good cause is shown for the failure to comply with the bylaw during the specified period. VOTED
  37|                              UNANIMOUSLY as printed in the warrant. (The only change from Zoning to General Bylaw is italicized in section
  38|                              C. Applicability)
  39|                       Meeting Adjourned at 10:02 P.M.
  40|                                                                                              185
